Demand Transparency. The system must be visible. The decisions must be public. The opacity must be broken. If you cannot see the extraction, you cannot stop it. The question to ask: Who profits? Follow the money. If the extraction is happening, someone is benefiting. Find them.


Demand Sunset Clauses. All emergency powers, laws, and "temporary" measures must automatically expire. No permanence by default. If the extraction cannot be locked in, the trap fails. The question to ask: Does this measure have an expiration date? If it does not, assume the worst.


Demand the Opposition. Every "crisis" proposal must first articulate the strongest opposing argument. If the crisis is manufactured, the opposition exposes it. Build a permanent, adversarial body whose job is to find the extraction in every panic. The question to ask: What is the strongest argument against this? If no one is making it, assume it is being suppressed.


Demand the Right to Exit. Those affected by the "crisis" must have a dignified exit. If the threat is real, they can adapt. If it's fake, they have the resources to walk away. The question to ask: Can I leave if I disagree? If the door is locked, assume the trap is set.


Build the Bench. In every district, a bench with a question carved into the wall: Is this enough? When people are forced to ask the question, the trap fails. The question to ask: Is this enough? Not for the system. For you. If the answer is no, resist.


Quick Reference: How to Spot the Next Trap
When the crisis is declared, look for the signs. The first stage is the Scare. Listen for the language: "We must act now!" "Unprecedented!" Ask yourself: Is the threat real, or is it vague and unproven? The second stage is the Snatch. Watch for rapid changes with no debate. Ask yourself: Who profits immediately? Follow the money. The third stage is the Fade. Notice when the crisis disappears and the media moves on. Ask yourself: What happened to the threat? Did it vanish? The fourth stage is the Lock. Observe when "temporary" measures become permanent. Ask yourself: Is there a sunset clause? Can it expire?


The Final Truth
The trap is not accidental. It is built into the architecture of power. The extraction machine produces crises, extracts resources, and locks in the extraction. This is not a bug. This is the operating system. But the trap is not eternal. It just thinks it is. The structural conditions that enable it can be dismantled. The immune system can be built. The bench can be installed. The question is whether you will build it. Whether you will ask the question. Whether you will refuse to be trapped. The extraction machine is not eternal. It just thinks it is. Once you see the pattern, you cannot unsee it. The question is whether you will let it trap you.
High Markup Scams
High Markup Scams
Dietary Supplements
The supplement industry is a $50 billion market constructed on hope and dread. The script is straightforward: "You're running on empty. Your body craves these vitamins, minerals, and extracts." The problem is fabricated. You're persuaded that something essential is missing. Without these pills, exhaustion, frailty, and decay await. The answer is the bottle. The guarantee is renewal: vigor, wellness, longevity, strength. The extraction hides in the price tag. Manufacturing costs hover under one dollar. Store shelves display twenty to fifty dollars. The gap is pure seizure.


The truth is starker. Most supplements escape meaningful oversight. The FDA doesn't verify safety or effectiveness before release. Many harbor fillers, toxic metals, or zero active ingredients. A 2015 probe revealed herbal products contained none of the advertised botanicals. Cheap substitutes like rice powder and soybean dominated. The extraction remains invisible because buyers believe they're purchasing health. The placebo effect seals the trap. Feeling better follows from expecting to feel better. Purchases continue. Extraction persists.


Beauty and Skincare Products
The beauty industry thrives on fabricated inadequacy. The message is clear: "You fall short. This cream, this serum, this lotion will correct your flaws." The crisis is manufactured. Skin ages. Pores clog. Wrinkles appear. Without intervention, ugliness, rejection, and invisibility loom. The urgency feeds on fear of time and judgment. The product offers deliverance. The promise shimmers: youth, allure, confidence, affection. The extraction appears as the price. Production costs amount to pocket change. Retail demands fifty to two hundred dollars. The leap is staggering.


The substance tells another tale. Most creams contain water, glycerin, and fragrance. Active components appear in homeopathic traces that achieve nothing. Cosmetics escape pharmaceutical regulation. Vague assertions require no proof. The extraction stays hidden because buyers think they're investing in appearance. Routine locks the cage. Daily application breeds belief. Products keep selling. Extraction keeps flowing.


Homeopathic "Remedies"
Homeopathy operates on the notion that "like cures like." The pitch is seductive: "This minuscule pellet holds the spirit of the remedy, diluted beyond existence." The deficiency is invented. Your system is unbalanced. Without this cure, suffering, fatigue, and agony persist. The urgency springs from dread of sickness and distress. The pellet provides relief. The promise whispers: healing, restoration, tranquility, wholeness. The extraction hides in the cost. Sugar pills cost pennies. Stores charge ten to thirty dollars. The profit is sheer fantasy.


The science tells the truth. Homeopathic solutions are diluted to nothing. No molecule of the original substance remains. They are sugar. They achieve nothing. The extraction remains invisible because patients think they're treating illness. The placebo effect reinforces the delusion. Feeling improved follows from expecting improvement. Remedies keep selling. Extraction continues.

The promise gleams: speed, connectivity, prestige, protection. The extraction hides in the retail figure. Production costs run hundreds. Shelves display thousands. The margin is substantial.


The hardware proves the deception. Improvements are marginal. Devices are constructed to fail. Batteries are sealed. Screens resist repair. The extraction remains invisible because buyers think they're securing the future. Ecosystems tighten the noose. Brand loyalty locks customers in. Upgrades keep selling. Extraction continues.

The organic item is the salvation. The promise echoes: health, purity, security, peace. The extraction hides in the premium. Organic prices exceed conventional by fifty to one hundred percent. The markup is seizure.


The reality undermines the claim. Organic food offers no consistent health advantage. Environmental benefits remain questionable. Approved pesticides can still be harmful. The extraction stays hidden because buyers think they're protecting their families. Identity seals the trap. The organic consumer becomes the label. Purchases keep flowing. Extraction persists.

Your spirit is blocked, your mind races, your body rebels. Without these oils, unrest, sickness, and emptiness consume you. The urgency builds on fear of imbalance. The oil is the cure. Without alkaline hydration, fatigue, disease, and decay follow. The urgency feeds on fear of internal disorder. The bottle is the remedy. The promise glows: health, energy, balance, vitality. Choosing the Worst Option
Choosing the Worst Option
It is not that we lack knowledge of alternatives. It is that we select the destructive path because the extraction machine profits from destruction. The pattern is invariant. A harmful practice generates revenue. A superior alternative exists. It is cleaner, healthier, more sustainable, and frequently cheaper over time. The harmful practice endures regardless. Those who benefit control the narrative, the infrastructure, and the policy. This is not ignorance. This is structural capture.


Plastic Packaging vs. Reusables
The Harmful Practice
Single-use plastic packaging. It inundates landfills, strangles marine life, and sheds microplastics into our bloodstreams. The devastation is apparent. Refuse accumulates everywhere. The burden is shifted onto ecosystems and descendants.


The Alternative
Reusable vessels, glass, metal, and compostable substitutes. They are robust, non-toxic, and genuinely recyclable. They endure for years. They do not contaminate the earth. They are the obvious selection.


Why We Continue
Plastic is inexpensive to manufacture. The fossil fuel sector has staked its future on plastic production. As energy markets transform, petrochemical corporations have designated plastic as their primary growth vehicle. They campaign against restrictions. They propagate recycling falsehoods. They finance initiatives that assign blame to consumers for the waste. The extraction is entrenched. The manufacturing base is established. The distribution networks are solidified. Path dependency is absolute.


Private Cars vs. Public Transit
The Harmful Practice
Individual automobile ownership. It generates congestion, emissions, sedentarism, and social fragmentation. It consumes enormous territory for thoroughfares and parking structures. It fractures neighborhoods. It claims millions of lives through collisions and pollution.


The Alternative
Mass transit, bicycle networks, and pedestrian infrastructure. They are cleaner, healthier, and more economical. They alleviate gridlock. They foster communal bonds. They are the sensible choice.


Why We Continue
The automotive and petroleum industries have invested a century constructing infrastructure tailored to cars. Suburbs are configured for vehicles, not inhabitants. Zoning ordinances mandate parking provisions. Roadways are prioritized over cycle lanes. The default is driving. The alternative is sidelined. Path dependency is complete. Extraction persists.


Factory Farming vs. Regenerative Agriculture
The Harmful Practice
Confined Animal Feeding Operations. They foster antibiotic resistance, generate pollution, and inflict massive suffering on sentient beings. They deplete topsoil. They poison waterways. They constitute a public health catastrophe in gestation.


The Alternative
Regenerative husbandry, pasture-raised livestock, and plant-based nutrition. They are more wholesome, more sustainable, and frequently more lucrative for cultivators. They restore soil fertility. They capture carbon. They honor animal welfare.


Why We Continue
Factory farming receives subsidies. Agribusiness conglomerates dominate the supply chain. The price of inexpensive meat excludes the expense of ecological degradation and public wellness. The extraction is enormous. The burdens are externalized. The industry wields political influence. Reform is obstructed.


Coal Power vs. Renewables
The Harmful Practice
Combusting coal for electricity generation. It discharges airborne toxins, accelerates climate disruption, and triggers respiratory illness. It contaminates communities. It scars landscapes. It ranks as the filthiest energy source.


The Alternative
Solar arrays, wind turbines, and nuclear fission. They are purer, more affordable, and more enduring. They emit no greenhouse gases. They are the evident preference.


Why We Continue
Coal is deeply embedded. The industry commands political leverage. Transition demands workforce retraining and infrastructure retirement. Extraction proves lucrative in the immediate horizon. Expenses are postponed. The industry contests every initiative for change. Path dependency is absolute.


Single-Use Coffee Cups vs. Reusables
The Harmful Practice
Disposable coffee cups. They are coated with plastic, cannot be recycled, and accumulate in landfills. They are utilized for moments and endure for centuries.


The Alternative
Reusable mugs. They are resilient, affordable, and broadly accessible. They last for years. They are the obvious selection.


Why We Continue
Convenience. The cost is shifted elsewhere. The consumer never witnesses the refuse. The extraction is invisible. The default is disposable. The infrastructure is constructed for single-use. The habit is cemented.


Paper Statements vs. Digital
The Harmful Practice
Paper bank statements, invoices, and unsolicited mail. They squander timber, energy, and water. They overcrowd mailboxes. They are anachronistic.


The Alternative
Electronic statements. They are instantaneous, paper-free, and more protected. They are the obvious choice.


Why We Continue
The paper sector possesses vested interests. Some consumers are sluggish to transition. The extraction is institutionalized. The infrastructure is established. The default is paper. The shift is gradual.


Burning Crop Residue vs. No-Till Farming
The Harmful Practice
Farmers ignite crop remnants post-harvest. This discharges airborne pollutants, provokes respiratory ailments, and releases carbon. It squanders organic material. It deteriorates soil quality.


The Alternative
No-till cultivation, cover cropping, and composting. They enhance soil vitality and diminish emissions. They are sustainable. They are the rational preference.


Why We Continue
Burning is inexpensive and straightforward. The alternative demands capital and instruction. The extraction is instantaneous. The expense is deferred. The default is burning. The transition is sluggish.


Bottom Trawling vs. Sustainable Fishing
The Harmful Practice
Bottom trawling drags massive weighted nets across the seafloor. The nets, some as large as twelve Boeing 747s, scrape the ocean bottom bare. A single pass can destroy structures that took thousands of years to form—coral reefs, sponge fields, and complex habitats that sustain marine life.


Up to 75% of marine life caught in bottom trawling nets dies and is discarded. Dolphins, seals, and sharks are caught indiscriminately. One in seven fish species caught by bottom trawlers are threatened or near threatened with extinction. The churning of seafloor sediment releases up to 370 million metric tons of carbon dioxide into the atmosphere every year—as much as the entire aviation industry and double the annual emissions from fuel combustion of the entire global fishing fleet.


The Alternative
Sustainable fishing methods: hook-and-line, traps, and selective gear that target specific species without destroying the habitat. Marine Protected Areas with genuine enforcement. The ecosystem is allowed to recover. The fish populations are sustained.


Why We Continue
Bottom trawling is kept alive by public money. Governments across Europe subsidize it to the tune of €1.3 billion every year. A 2026 study found that bottom trawling imposes up to €16 billion annually in net costs to society—costs 90 times greater than the €180 million in profits it generates. It is not just an environmental disaster; it is an economic failure.


The industry is protected by a revolving door. Governments reject outright bans. In the UK, bottom trawling is only completely banned in three Highly Protected Marine Areas; it remains allowed in more than 75% of other Marine Protected Areas. The "paper parks" exist on maps but are not enforced.


The extraction is locked in by path dependence. The ships, the nets, the supply chains, the ports—all are already built. The jobs depend on them. The path of least resistance is continuation. The industry benefits from "subsidy opacity"—no complete, centralized record of all public money going to fishing fleets.


The C.A.S.T. Protocol in All These Examples
Every detrimental practice follows identical structural logic.


The Crisis is the narrative: "We require plastic to preserve edibles. We require automobiles to commute. We require inexpensive meat to sustain the populace. We require coal to maintain illumination. We require bottom trawling to feed the world."


The Acquisition is the extraction. Gains are privatized. Burdens are externalized.


The Subsidence is the normalization. Society accepts the practice as ordinary. "That is merely how existence operates." Alternatives are marginalized.


The Trap is the lock-in. Infrastructure is erected. Path dependency is finalized. Extraction becomes perpetual.




Assassination
Assassination
"Assassination—the politically motivated killing of high-profile individuals—has always been part of the struggle for power, from the removal of tyrants in Classical civilizations to the state-sanctioned 'decapitation strikes' of the present day."


What Defines a Political Assassination?
A political assassination is the deliberate, extralegal killing of an individual for political purposes. It differs from ordinary murder in its motivation and target: it entails the premeditated killing of a prominent figure, such as a government leader or other influential person.


Assassination is necessarily political murder, but not all political murder is assassination. Political assassination typically involves the murder of a specific politician, and it falls between the cracks of electoral violence, civil war, and criminal violence. The targeted killing is intended to achieve political ends—to seize power, start a revolution, draw attention to a cause, exact revenge, or undermine a regime.


Part One: Why Assassinations Happen
The Motivational Spectrum
Political assassinations are not random acts of folly which defy analysis—they are well-planned and discriminate acts of violence. There is no single profile of a political assassin, and simplistic stereotypes are misleading.


The motivations fall into several overlapping categories:


Elite Substitution: The assassination of a political leader to replace him or her with another ruler. This is the oldest form—removing one powerful figure to install another.


Tyrannicide: The killing of a tyrant to liberate the people. Justified as removing an oppressive ruler for the greater good.


Terroristic Assassination: Political murder carried out to instill fear and advance a political agenda.


Propaganda by Deed: Assassination as a revolutionary act meant to inspire others and draw attention to a cause. The act itself is the message.


Anomic Assassination: Political murder driven by personal grievance, mental illness, or social dislocation rather than organized political goals.


The Psychology of the Assassin
Contemporary research challenges the assumption that assassinations are driven primarily by fear, anxiety, or anger. A 2026 theoretical analysis suggests that political assassinations are often more directly driven by disgust once a target is construed as unchangeable moral taint. The assassin sees the target not merely as an opponent but as something fundamentally corrupted—a moral stain that must be removed.


Institutional and Structural Factors
The institutional context matters. Research on political assassinations since World War II has found that institutional factors related to leadership succession, institutionalized power, and levels of repression interact to influence the occurrence of such killings. Repressive leaders are at greater risk for assassination, though the effect is moderated by other factors.


Assassination has emerged in waves during periods of political upheaval and ideological conflict. It is more frequent in certain types of societies than others.


Part Two: How Assassinations Are Carried Out
The Arsenal of Methods
Assassins have been killing the powerful and famous for at least three thousand years. The methods have evolved but follow recognizable patterns.


Traditional Weapons: Daggers and poisons have been the favored tools of assassins, and they remain popular despite the greater efficiency of bombs and bullets. Julius Caesar was stabbed to death by a group of Roman senators. Leon Trotsky was killed with an ice axe wielded by a Stalinist agent.


Firearms: Bullets have become the modern assassin's primary tool. From the rifle that killed Abraham Lincoln to the handgun used on Yitzhak Rabin to the automatic rifles used in the assassination of Anwar Sadat, firearms provide range and relative ease of use.


Explosives: Car bombs, letter bombs, and other explosives have been used to kill political figures. The assassination of Benazir Bhutto involved a shooter who then detonated explosives.


Poison: A CIA 1954 manual listed a plethora of methods including poisons and contrived accidents. The polonium poisoning of Alexander Litvinenko in 2006 and the ricin assassination of Georgi Markov in 1978 using a poisoned umbrella demonstrate the enduring appeal of chemical and biological methods.


State-Sponsored Methods: State-sponsored assassinations employ the full range of techniques. The CIA's 1954 manual, A Study in Assassination, listed methods that the US government could consider using. These include everything from sniper rifles to poison to "contrived accidents".


State-Sponsored Assassination
State-sponsored assassinations are carried out covertly and are not officially recognized by their sponsor. The state maintains plausible deniability while achieving its political objectives.


The US government has a long history of involvement in assassinations and assassination attempts, from Fidel Castro to Qassem Soleimani in 2020. The methods have ranged from poison to sniper rifles to drone strikes.


Other states have employed death squads. Spain's GAL (Anti-terrorist Liberation Groups) was a state-sponsored death squad responsible for 28 murders between 1983 and 1987, with orders coming from the highest levels of government.


Part Three: Theories of Assassination
The Lone Assassin vs. The Conspiracy
A fundamental question in assassination studies: Are political murders usually produced by elaborate conspiracies, or are they more often the work of lone assassins?


The Lone Assassin Theory: Some assassinations are the work of individuals acting alone. Lee Harvey Oswald is the official lone assassin of JFK. James Earl Ray was convicted as the lone assassin of Martin Luther King Jr.. Yigal Amir acted alone in killing Yitzhak Rabin.


The Conspiracy Theory: Many assassinations generate theories of wider conspiracies. The JFK assassination has produced decades of theories involving the CIA, the Mafia, Castro, Israel, and Lyndon Johnson. The MLK assassination has generated theories of government and Mafia involvement. The RFK assassination continues to attract speculation about whether Sirhan Sirhan was a pawn in a larger conspiracy.


The Pattern of Conspiracy Theories: Assassination has long been fertile ground for misinformation, disinformation, propaganda, and conspiracy theories. The assassination of JFK sparked theories that Oswald did not act alone, that Fidel Castro was behind the assassination, and that the "Deep State" was involved. The assassination of Swedish Prime Minister Olof Palme also generated conspiracy theories.


The Deep State Theory
The "Deep State" theory posits that a hidden network of intelligence agencies, military officials, and political elites orchestrates political assassinations to maintain power. Theories about the Deep State being behind the assassinations of JFK and the 9/11 attacks are persistent. Similar theories emerged around the 2024 assassination attempt on Slovak PM Robert Fico.


Conspiracy as Structural Symptom
Conspiracy theories flourish where opacity is high. When governments classify documents, when investigations are incomplete, when official narratives have inconsistencies, the public fills the gaps with the most dramatic story available. The JFK assassination remains a fertile ground for theories because the US government withheld documents for decades. The recent release of declassified files has added fuel rather than extinguishing the fire.


Part Four: Assassinations That Changed History
The Ancient World: Julius Caesar (44 BC)
Julius Caesar was assassinated by a conspiracy of approximately forty Roman senators who called themselves Liberatores (Liberators), led by Gaius Cassius Longinus and Marcus Junius Brutus.


The Motive: Fear of his power and domination of the state. Caesar had been proclaimed "dictator for life". The senators feared he wished to crown himself king of Rome. Some historians argue the Senate aristocrats killed Caesar because they perceived him as a popular leader who threatened their privileged interests.


The Method: Multiple stab wounds. Caesar was stabbed to death in the Theatre of Pompey on the Ides of March (March 15) 44 BC.


The Consequence: The Republic was never fully restored. Caesar's death led to civil war and eventually the Roman Empire under his adopted heir Augustus. The assassination failed to preserve the Republic and accelerated its destruction.


The Catalyst: Archduke Franz Ferdinand (1914)
The assassination of Archduke Franz Ferdinand of Austria by Gavrilo Princip, a 19-year-old Serbian nationalist, is arguably the most consequential political murder in modern history.


The Motive: To free Bosnia of Austria-Hungarian rule and establish a common South Slav ("Yugoslav") state. Austria-Hungary had annexed Bosnia-Herzegovina from the declining Ottoman Empire in 1908.


The Method: A pistol fired at close range in Sarajevo.


The Consequence: The assassination precipitated the July Crisis, leading Austria-Hungary to declare war on Serbia, triggering a chain reaction of alliances that started World War I. Four weeks after the assassination, the major European powers were at war.


The State-Sponsored Assassination: Leon Trotsky (1940)
Leon Trotsky was assassinated in Mexico City by Ramón Mercader, a Spanish communist recruited by Stalin's NKVD.


The Motive: Stalin feared that Trotsky might organize a movement to overthrow the new ruling elite in the Soviet Union. Trotsky had been exiled since 1929 and continued to criticize Soviet leadership.


The Method: An ice axe—a mountaineer's pickaxe—struck into Trotsky's skull. Stalin had previously tried to assassinate Trotsky using machine guns.


The Consequence: The assassination eliminated Stalin's most prominent critic and consolidated his grip on power. Trotsky's Fourth International never recovered.


The Assassination of a Dictator: Rafael Trujillo (1961)
Rafael Trujillo, the dictator of the Dominican Republic, was gunned down by assassins in May 1961.


The Motive: After 31 years of harsh rule, a group of Dominican dissidents, backed by the CIA, sought to remove him. The Eisenhower administration wanted Trujillo removed.


The Method: Guns—apparently fast-firing M-1 carbines smuggled into the Caribbean island by CIA operatives. Declassified documents confirm that the CIA provided material support to the conspirators, including the supply of weapons through the US embassy.


The Consequence: Trujillo's assassination failed to produce the political transformation his killers had hoped for. The CIA had actually been involved in three separate plots to kill the dictator.


The Assassination of a Revolutionary Leader: Patrice Lumumba (1961)
Patrice Lumumba, the first Prime Minister of the Democratic Republic of Congo, was assassinated within a year of taking office.


The Motive: The CIA wanted Lumumba removed. The assassination of Lumumba is "as close as we come to a Presidential order to assassinate," with the order executed at all levels of the Agency's hierarchy, from Director Allen Dulles to the CIA Chief of Station in Congo. Lumumba's nationalist and anti-colonial stance threatened Western interests in the region.


The Method: Lumumba was deposed in a CIA-backed coup, transferred to enemy territory in a CIA-approved operation, and shot dead by Congolese assassins.


The Consequence: The assassination of Lumumba set the Congo on a path of instability, dictatorship, and resource extraction that continues to shape the region.


Part Five: The American Era (1960s)
John F. Kennedy (1963)
The assassination of President John F. Kennedy in Dallas, Texas, on November 22, 1963, is the most scrutinized political murder in American history.


The Official Narrative: Lee Harvey Oswald acted alone. The Warren Commission concluded Oswald fired three shots from the Texas School Book Depository.


The Conspiracy Theories: For more than 60 years, the CIA claimed it had little or no knowledge of Oswald's activities before the assassination. Newly declassified documents continue to add fuel to questions about what the CIA knew. Theories include CIA involvement, Mafia involvement, Castro's involvement, Israeli involvement (linked to Israel's nuclear program), and Lyndon Johnson's involvement.


The Consequence: The assassination traumatized a nation, generated decades of conspiracy theories, and transformed how Americans view their government. Political assassinations emerged in waves during periods of political upheaval, and the 1960s saw multiple assassinations in the US.


Martin Luther King Jr. (1968)
Martin Luther King Jr. was assassinated in Memphis, Tennessee, on April 4, 1968.


The Official Narrative: James Earl Ray was convicted as the lone assassin, acting out of racial animosity. Ray pleaded guilty but later retracted his confession.


The Conspiracy Theories: Theories have covered the gamut: multiple shooters, government or police involvement, Mafia influence. King's own family and the jurors of a 1999 civil court case have questioned the official explanation. Investigative researchers have described a tangled web of plots behind King's murder dating back to the late 1950s.


Robert F. Kennedy (1968)
Senator Robert F. Kennedy was assassinated on June 5, 1968, in Los Angeles, shortly after winning the California Democratic primary.


The Official Narrative: Sirhan Sirhan, a 24-year-old Palestinian, was convicted of first-degree murder. Sirhan admitted at his trial that he shot RFK but said he could not recall doing so.


The Conspiracy Theories: RFK's assassination, much like his brother's, has been the subject of much speculation. Theories include CIA involvement, Mafia involvement (RFK had continued his war against the Mafia), and Sirhan being an "involuntary pawn". Declassified records have revealed no evidence of a wider conspiracy. RFK Jr. has expressed doubts about Sirhan being the shooter and called for a reinvestigation.


Part Six: Assassinations in the Middle East and South Asia
Anwar Sadat (1981)
Egyptian President Anwar Sadat was assassinated on October 6, 1981, during the annual victory parade in Cairo celebrating the Yom Kippur War.


The Motive: Sadat's assassination was undertaken by members of the Egyptian Islamic Jihad. He was targeted for making peace with Israel—the Camp David Accords—which Islamists viewed as a betrayal. The assassination was part of a larger plan by Islamists to take over power in Egypt.


The Method: Automatic rifles fired at Sadat during the parade. The attack lasted about two minutes. Sadat died from bullet wounds to his aorta, along with intestinal and neck wounds.


The Consequence: Sadat's assassination was not a one-off event but part of a larger trend of Islamist political violence that continues to poison the region.


Indira Gandhi (1984)
Indian Prime Minister Indira Gandhi was assassinated on October 31, 1984, by two of her Sikh bodyguards.


The Motive: Retaliation for Operation Blue Star. In June 1984, Gandhi ordered the Indian Army to enter the Golden Temple in Amritsar, the Sikhs' holiest shrine, to remove militant Sikh separatists. The operation severely damaged Gandhi's standing with Sikhs.


The Method: Two of her trusted bodyguards, seeking retribution for the events at the temple, shot her outside her home. Gandhi had planned to reassign the Sikh bodyguards but canceled the transfer.


The Consequence: The assassination led to anti-Sikh riots in which approximately 10,000 Sikhs were killed. The assassination strengthened the Sikh insurgency in Punjab and led to state-sponsored repression.


Benazir Bhutto (2007)
Pakistan's former Prime Minister Benazir Bhutto was assassinated on December 27, 2007, in Rawalpindi.


The Motive: Bhutto was a political rival who had returned to Pakistan to contest elections. Theories include Taliban involvement, elements within the government, and intelligence agencies.


The Method: An assassin shot Bhutto in the neck and then blew himself up, killing 24 people.


The Theories: Musharraf's government blamed the assassination on Pakistani Taliban chief Baitullah Mehsud, who denied involvement. The theories remain unresolved.


Yitzhak Rabin (1995)
Israeli Prime Minister Yitzhak Rabin was assassinated on November 4, 1995, after a peace rally in Tel Aviv.


The Motive: The assassination sought to change government policy and prevent the implementation of the Oslo Accords. Yigal Amir, a Jewish extremist, opposed the Oslo Accords, which involved ceding territory to the PLO in exchange for peace.


The Method: A handgun. Amir shot Rabin twice. He had attended multiple public events where Rabin was present, waiting for the perfect moment.


The Consequence: The assassination is considered "one of the most successful political killings of the 20th Century"—it killed the peace process. The assassin achieved his political objective: destroying the Oslo Accords.


Part Seven: Does Assassination Work?
The Mixed Record
The complete efficacy of assassination has not been established. The results are mixed. As one historian argues, individual assassinations have rarely changed the geopolitical weather except as catalysts in situations already in crisis.


Assassination is not so much a blunt tool as an unpredictable and often ineffective one.


Decapitation as Disruption, Not Transformation
Strategic analysis suggests that decapitation—removing a leader—is a tool of disruption not of transformation. It becomes a strategic error when leaders treat it as the latter. Assassinations give a short-term political boost but lead to long-term disaster. Killing an enemy leader might give a quick boost of popularity amid war, but the underlying political dynamics remain unchanged.


The Paradox of Political Murder
Assassination can be effective in some contexts. As one scholar notes, assassination "in many parts of the world has not only been a normal political act, rational, explicable, but also often effective, in some cases taking fewer lives in the transfer of power than an election".


But the long-term consequences are often counterproductive. The assassination of Rabin killed the peace process. The assassination of Lumumba destabilized the Congo. The assassination of Caesar accelerated the fall of the Republic. The assassination of Franz Ferdinand triggered a world war.


Part Eight: The Future of Assassination
An Increasingly Uninhibited Instrument
Analysts conclude that the use of assassination as a political tool is likely to become more widespread in the coming years. The normalization of targeted killing has blurred the line between assassination and legitimate military action.


The Normalization of Targeted Killing
From Fidel Castro to Qassem Soleimani, the US government has been involved in an array of assassinations and assassination attempts against foreign leaders and officials. The drone war has made targeted killing routine. The CIA's involvement in assassination attempts continues to be documented through declassified files.


The Information Environment
Conspiracy theories about assassinations will continue to flourish in an environment of opacity and polarization. Disinformation, propaganda, and conspiracy theories about political assassinations are increasingly common. The Deep State theory persists. New theories emerge with each new assassination attempt.
